# Inter-service contracts

Every way two components of the gateway talk to each other, with the source of truth for each contract. A contract is **IMPLEMENTED** when the code and a test exist in this repository, and **PLANNED x.y** when it is designed but not built (the step is from [`docs/plan/execution-plan.md`](../plan/execution-plan.md)). Breaking a contract requires a new version (events: `.v2`; HTTP: `/v2`), with both versions served during the migration (ADR-0005, carried forward by ADR-0024).

## 1. Postfix → smtp-adapter policy delegation

Postfix's [SMTP access policy delegation](http://www.postfix.org/SMTPD_POLICY_README.html) over TCP (`check_policy_service { inet:${SMTP_ADAPTER_HOST}:10040, timeout=10s, default_action=DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.3.5 …, policy_context=<context> }`). **IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2** ([design D4, D6, D11](../plan/steps/04-smtp-gateway.md)). Every listener passes its own `policy_context`; an unknown or missing context is `DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.3.5` (a misconfiguration never opens anything).

| Context             | Listener                                | Checks at `RCPT` (first non-pass wins)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `inbound`           | `postfix` 25 (also at `END-OF-MESSAGE`) | recipient domain active + `inbound_enabled` (`REJECT 5.7.1 Relay access denied`), per-client-IP and per-tenant recipient rates (`DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.7.1`), recipient validation (`REJECT 5.1.1` only in authoritative allow-list mode), declared size vs tenant limit (`REJECT 5.3.4`); at end of data the recorded per-instance size limit                                                                                   |
| `submission`        | `postfix-outbound` 587                  | SASL connector active (`REJECT 5.7.1 Relay access denied`), sender domain of the connector's organization and outbound-enabled (`REJECT 5.7.1 Sender address not authorized`), active DKIM key when `SMTP_OUTBOUND_REQUIRE_DKIM` (`REJECT 5.7.1 Sender domain is not ready …`), suppression (`REJECT 5.7.1 Recipient address suppressed`), per-tenant per-minute/day limits (`DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.7.1`), size (`REJECT 5.3.4`) |
| `relay`             | `postfix-outbound` 25                   | as `submission`, connector from `ccert_fingerprint` (wins) or `client_address`; `require_tls` without TLS → `REJECT 5.7.1 Must issue a STARTTLS command first`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `reinject-inbound`  | `postfix-outbound` 10026                | recipient domain must be an active tenant domain (`REJECT 5.7.1 Relay access denied`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `reinject-outbound` | `postfix-outbound` 10027                | sender domain active + outbound-enabled (`REJECT 5.7.1 Sender address not authorized`), suppression, DKIM readiness `HEXISTS dkim_selectors <domain>` on redis-dkim (missing or error → `DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.7.0 Signing key not available`)                                                                                                                                                                                   |

Postfix renders a policy `REJECT` as `550 5.7.1 …` / `550 5.1.1 …` (verified by the stack suites); the listener's own final `reject` answers `554 5.7.1 … Access denied`. Any thrown error, Redis or DB failure, or a decision slower than `SMTP_POLICY_DECISION_TIMEOUT_MS` answers `DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.3.5 Policy service temporarily unavailable`. **The adapter never answers `OK`.** Replies are generic: no tenant names, ids or internal errors.

**Tenant resolution query.** `resolveActiveDomain(db, rawName)` (`packages/database/src/domains/resolve-active-domain.ts`) maps a recipient (or sender) domain to its organization in-session: normalized like the API, read by the unique `verified_name` on the **writer**, `active` domain of an `active` organization only. The adapter caches the domain view in Redis (`sa:dom:*`, 60 s, negative 30 s) and drops entries when it sees `domain.status.changed.v1` in the outbox tail (≤ 5 s; the TTL is the guarantee). Post-queue ingestion uses `resolveDomainOwner` (also `suspended` domains/organizations: mail accepted before a suspension is still stored).

**Wire behaviour** (`policy-protocol.ts`, `policy-server.service.ts`): requests may span TCP chunks and be pipelined; replies are written strictly in request order; a request over **16 KiB** or a line without `=` is a protocol error (tempfail, then close); an action containing CR or LF is refused; idle connections are closed after 60 s. Health: `policy-listener`.

**Postfix side**: `smtpd_policy_service_timeout = 10s`, default action `DEFER_IF_PERMIT 4.3.5 Policy service unavailable`. Unreachable or slow means **4xx, never accept-all**. Tested by `tests/integration/smtp-fail-closed.test.ts` (Redis down; adapter container stopped in CI) and `mail-plane.test.ts` (no adapter).

## 2. Postfix → Rspamd milter

Inbound policy since step 4.1: [ADR-0027](adr/0027-rspamd-milter-policy.md), [S11](../plan/steps/09-rspamd.md#s11-rspamd-at-smtp-time-milter-adr-0027), [deployment](../deployment/rspamd.md). The outbound `rspamd-outbound` DKIM signer is unchanged (ADR-0020).

| Aspect    | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Transport | Milter protocol 6 to `inet:rspamd:11332` (Rspamd proxy worker in milter mode, `self_scan = yes`, `count = 1`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Macros    | connect `j {daemon_name} {daemon_addr} v _ {client_addr} {client_name}`; MAIL `i {auth_type} {auth_authen} {auth_author} {mail_addr} {mail_host} {mail_mailer} {client_addr} {client_name}`; RCPT `i {rcpt_addr} {rcpt_host} {rcpt_mailer}`; end of data `i`                                                                                     |
| Timeouts  | Postfix connect 10 s, command 10 s, content 30 s; Rspamd proxy timeout 25 s, `task_timeout` 15 s                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Actions   | `554 5.7.1` only for GTUBE and score ≥ `RSPAMD_MILTER_REJECT_SCORE` (15); no `add_header`, subject rewrite, greylisting or soft reject. DMARC `p=reject` only with `RSPAMD_MILTER_DMARC_REJECT=true` (default off). Everything else is classified post-queue (§22)                                                                               |
| Headers   | Removes every inbound `X-Spam*`, `X-Spamd-*`, `X-Rspamd-*` and the `Authentication-Results` carrying our authserv-id (`RSPAMD_AUTHSERV_ID` = Postfix `myhostname`; custom routine `eg-strip-forged`), adds exactly one `Authentication-Results` of ours; no score header. Foreign `Authentication-Results` are kept and never trusted downstream |
| Failure   | `milter_default_action = accept`: Rspamd down or slow → the message is accepted without the SMTP-time pass and always scanned post-queue, where a failed engine is `scan_failed`, never clean. The policy service still fails closed                                                                                                             |

Verified through Postfix by `tests/integration/scan-smtp.test.ts` (GTUBE `554 5.7.1`, nothing stored; forged headers removed) and `scan-resilience.test.ts` (Rspamd stopped: `250` accepted).

## 3. Postfix → smtp-adapter LMTP ingestion

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2** ([design D7, D8, D13](../plan/steps/04-smtp-gateway.md); MIME parsing and `email_headers`/`email_attachments` rows are step 3.1). LMTP server built on `smtp-server` (`lmtp: true`, XFORWARD, no AUTH/STARTTLS), only for peers inside `SMTP_INTERNAL_CLIENT_NETWORKS`.

| Aspect       | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Listeners    | `:10050` = `direction inbound` (`postfix` `relay_transport`), `:10051` = `direction outbound` (`postfix-outbound` `content_filter` of 587 and 25), `:10052` = bounce notices. **The port decides the direction**, never content                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Envelope     | Inbound: each recipient's organization = `resolveDomainOwner(recipient domain)`; no owner → that recipient `451 4.3.0`. Outbound: the organization of the envelope sender domain (the policy proved the connector belongs to it). XFORWARD `IDENT` (the queue id) is required                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Ingest key   | Per organization: `v2:` + hex SHA-256 of `{mtaId}\|{queueId}\|{direction}\|{contentSha256}\|{recipientSetSha256}` (`mtaId` = LHLO name; the recipient set = that organization's accepted addresses, lower-cased, de-duplicated, sorted, LF-joined). A retry after a lost `250` carries the same queue file and recipients → same key → `250` without new rows (stack suite 7). A retry of only the recipients that were deferred (one RCPT tempfailed while another of the same organization was stored) has another key and is stored as its own message, never answered `250` and lost. `v1:` keys (no recipient set) predate the step 2.2 review fix |
| Transactions | **One writer transaction per organization**: `message_identities`, `email_messages` (`status received`, header metadata, `eml_object_key`, `eml_sha256`, `mta_queue_id`), `email_recipients`, one `email_events` `received`, one `message_outbox` `mail.message.received.v1` (validated)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Replies      | Per recipient in RCPT order: `250 2.0.0 Ok` after its organization's commit (or duplicate); `451 4.3.0 Temporary failure, please retry` for any S3/DB/timeout failure of that organization. Organizations are independent; never `5xx` for infrastructure faults                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Refusals     | `554 5.4.6 Routing loop detected` (valid gateway trace for the same organization and direction, §16); outbound `550 5.7.1 From header does not match the authenticated sender domain`. Both become DSNs to an authenticated or traced sender (ADR-0019 exception)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Direction    | **Step 2.3 (R1):** per recipient `classifyRecipient` (`@email-gateway/mail-routing`) → `email_recipients.direction`. Inbound listener: always `inbound` (also for a sender in the organization's own domain). Outbound listener: `internal` when `resolveActiveDomain(R)` returns the same organization, else `outbound` (including another tenant's domain on this gateway). `email_messages.direction` = `internal` only when every recipient of that organization is internal; the `mail.message.received.v1` payload carries it. The ingest key, the S3 key and the loop check keep the **listener** direction                                      |
| Bodies       | Streamed once to S3 (multipart above 5 MiB, bounded memory), a server-side `CopyObject` per extra organization, never in MySQL                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

**Bounce notices (`:10052`).** `postfix-outbound` sends postmaster copies of its DSNs (`notify_classes = bounce, delay`) to `bounce-notices@${POSTFIX_BOUNCE_DOMAIN}`. That address is reserved: the policy refuses it (and its subdomains) as a recipient in every context (`550 5.7.1 Relay access denied`), and the handler refuses any LMTP envelope sender other than Postfix's bounce daemon (`<>` or `double-bounce@${POSTFIX_OUTBOUND_MYHOSTNAME}`, `550 5.7.1`). The handler parses `message/delivery-status`, resolves the organization from `X-Postfix-Sender`, and records the notice only if the undelivered message's headers (`text/rfc822-headers` part) carry a valid **outbound** `X-Gateway-Trace` of that organization (§16): only mail the gateway reinjected on 10027 for delivery says anything about the tenant. Failures of the ingestion hop (content filter LMTP 10051: From check, loop, adapter/S3/DB outage) and notices naming another tenant's sender domain are ignored (`250`, counted `ignored`). Per failed/delayed recipient it writes `bounce_events` (dedupe key SHA-256 of queue id, recipient, action, status) and, for hard address failures (`5.1.1`, `5.1.2`, `5.1.3`, `5.1.6`, `5.1.10`, `5.2.1`), upserts `delivery_suppressions` (`hard_bounce`, `source = bounce`, `SUPPRESSION_HARD_BOUNCE_DAYS`; never shortens a `manual`/`complaint` row). DB error → `451`; unparseable or unowned → `250` (counted).

**Step 2.3 (R9):** before the 2.2 rules, the handler matches the notice to a reinjection by `X-Postfix-Queue-ID` (= `delivery_reinjections.queue_id`) or `Original-Envelope-Id` (= the reinjection token, §15) and, when matched, records per recipient a `bounce_notice` delivery signal with the organization **of the reinjection row** (any direction, including inbound 10026 deliveries): `failed` + `5.x.x` → `bounced`, `failed` + `4.x.x` → `expired`, `delayed` → `deferred`. Signal key `dsn|{queueId}|{address}|{action}|{status}|{arrivalDate}`: a redelivered notice is a duplicate (no transition, no event, no outbox row). For matched outbound notices `bounce_events.message_id` / `message_received_at` are filled. Inbound and internal bounces create no suppressions and no reputation events.

## 4. RabbitMQ topology

**Topology v4 (step 3.2, [ADR-0024](adr/0024-rabbitmq-topology-retry-dead-letter.md); supersedes the ADR-0005 `mail.events`/`mail.dlx` topology; v2: step 3.3 [L7](../plan/steps/08-message-lifecycle.md#l7-events-packagesevent-contracts-registry-topology-v2), [ADR-0025](adr/0025-message-lifecycle-state-machine.md); v3: step 4.1 [S2](../plan/steps/09-rspamd.md#s2-what-starts-a-scan-and-through-which-queues-topology-v3), [ADR-0026](adr/0026-security-scan-pipeline.md); v4: step 4.2 [A2](../plan/steps/10-attachment-scanning.md#a2-topology-v4-and-the-sandbox-contract), [ADR-0028](adr/0028-attachment-scanning-engine.md); v5: step 4.3 [U2](../plan/steps/11-url-protection.md#u2-topology-v5-and-the-three-url-events), [ADR-0029](adr/0029-url-security-engine.md); v6: step 4.4 [B3](../plan/steps/12-phishing-bec.md#b3-topology-v6-and-grants), [ADR-0033](adr/0033-bec-engine-and-stage-dependencies.md); v7: step 4.5 [D2](../plan/steps/13-dlp.md#d2-topology-v7-and-grants), [ADR-0036](adr/0036-dlp-engine-and-extraction.md); v8: step 4.6 [X11](../plan/steps/14-sandbox.md#x11-topology-v8-events-and-broker-users-adr-0040-decision-2), [ADR-0040](adr/0040-sandbox-engine-dispatch-results-artifacts.md); v9: step 5.2 [P15](../plan/steps/16-policy-engine.md#p15-events-and-topology-v9-adr-0044-decision-6), [ADR-0044](adr/0044-policy-evaluation-placement-and-delivery-gating.md); v10: step 5.3 [Q27](../plan/steps/17-quarantine.md#q27-events-and-topology-v10-wp1), [ADR-0046](adr/0046-quarantine-store-and-per-recipient-decisions.md); v11: step 6.2 [§4.3](../plan/steps/19-webhooks-notifications.md#43-topology-v11-packagesevent-contractssrctopologyts-wp1), [ADR-0055](adr/0055-webhook-signing-and-delivery.md)).** **v11** (`TOPOLOGY_VERSION = 11`, step 6.2) adds the **work queue `email.webhooks`** of the webhook-worker's fanout (home exchange `email.events`; bindings `org.*.mail.message.received.v1` (email.ingestion), `org.*.mail.message.scanned.v1` and `org.*.mail.attachment.sandbox_completed.v1` (email.security), `org.*.mail.policy.decided.v1` (email.policy), `org.*.mail.delivery.approved.v1` (email.delivery), `org.*.mail.message.state_changed.v1`, `org.*.domain.status.changed.v1`, `org.*.domain.dns.records_changed.v1` and `retry.email.webhooks.#` (email.events); ledger `webhook-worker.fanout`, user `eg-webhook`; retry `hold` 10 / 2 s–1 min / 24 h; policy `work`) and binds `org.*.notification.alert_requested.v1` (email.policy) to `email.notifications`: 21 queues, 58 bindings, 10 role users. `email.webhooks` is **not** reconciled (`RECONCILE_QUEUES`): the fanout acks no-op events without a ledger row (ADR-0055 decision 13). **v10** (`TOPOLOGY_VERSION = 10`, step 5.3) turns the dormant `email.quarantine` into the **work queue of the policy-engine's release executor** (home exchange `email.policy`; bindings `org.*.mail.quarantine.release_requested.v1` and `retry.email.quarantine.#`; its v9 `mail.policy.decided.v1` binding is removed and pruned by `init.mjs`; ledger `policy-engine.quarantine`, user `eg-policy-engine`; retry `hold` 10 / 2 s–1 min / 24 h; policy `work`), binds `org.*.mail.quarantine.notification_requested.v1` to `email.notifications`, and registers both `mail.quarantine.*` events (§6, §29); `mail.policy.decided.v1` stays the analytics/firehose event — **20 quorum queues, 48 bindings**, nine role users (`eg-policy-engine` reads `email.policy.evaluate` and `email.quarantine`, `eg-ops` may replay `retry.email.quarantine.`). **v9** (`TOPOLOGY_VERSION = 9`, step 5.2) makes `email.policy.evaluate` the work queue of the policy-engine (ledger `policy-engine.evaluate`, user `eg-policy-engine`; bindings unchanged: `scanned.v1` on `email.security`, `parse_failed.v1` on `email.ingestion`, `retry.email.policy.evaluate.#`; retry `hold` 10 / 2 s–1 min / 24 h; policy `work`), adds the work queue `email.notifications` (home exchange `email.policy`, bound to `org.*.mail.policy.notification_requested.v1` and `retry.email.notifications.#`; ledger `notification-worker.policy`, user `eg-notification`; retry `hold` 8 / 5 s–1 min / 24 h), replaces the dormant `email.archive` binding `mail.policy.decided.v1` by `org.*.mail.message.archive_requested.v1` on `email.archive` (pruned by `init.mjs`) and registers `mail.policy.notification_requested.v1` and `mail.message.archive_requested.v1`; `mail.policy.decided.v1` is redefined in place (§6) — **20 quorum queues, 47 bindings**, nine role users. **v8** (`TOPOLOGY_VERSION = 8`) makes `scan.sandbox` the lossy work queue of the isolated sandbox-controller (`work-lossy`, consumer kind `stateless`, user `eg-sandbox`, bound to `org.*.mail.sandbox.task_requested.v1` and the qualified `retry.scan.sandbox.org.#`, retry `hold` 3 / 5 s–60 s / 1 h; its v1–v7 `sandbox_requested.v1` binding is pruned), adds `scan.sandbox.submissions` (`sandbox_requested.v1`, consumer `email-worker.scan.sandbox`) and `scan.sandbox.results` (`task_completed.v1`, consumer `email-worker.scan.sandbox.results`), both work queues of `eg-scanner` (retry `hold` 10 / 2 s–60 s / 24 h), and registers `mail.sandbox.task_requested.v1`, `mail.sandbox.task_completed.v1` (aggregate `sandbox_analysis`) and `mail.attachment.sandbox_completed.v1` (aggregate `attachment`; no queue binding) — **19 quorum queues, 45 bindings**, seven role users. **v7** makes `scan.dlp` a work queue (stage 1: bound to `parsed.v1`, `rescan_requested.v1` and `retry.scan.dlp.#`, consumer `email-worker.scan.dlp`, user `eg-scanner`, retry `hold` 10 / 2 s–60 s / 24 h) and registers `mail.message.dlp_incident.v1` (no queue binds it in 4.5; the `email.events` firehose forwards it to the dormant `email.analytics`; the notification-worker binds it in 6.2) — still 17 quorum queues and **41 bindings** (the dormant `scan.dlp` already had its retry binding; step 4.5 §12 WP1 deviation 1). **v6** adds the work queue `scan.bec` (stage 2: bound only to `org.*.mail.message.scan_stage_ready.v1` and `retry.scan.bec.#`, consumer `email-worker.scan.bec`, user `eg-scanner`) and registers `mail.message.scan_stage_ready.v1` — 17 quorum queues, 41 bindings; `scan.auth` stays reserved. **v5** makes `scan.url` a work queue (consumer `email-worker.scan.url`, user `eg-scanner`, retry binding `retry.scan.url.org.#`), adds `scan.url.inspect` (lossy work queue: `max-length 10 000`, `overflow drop-head`, consumer kind `stateless`, user `eg-url-browser`) and `scan.url.results` (consumer `email-worker.scan.url.results`) — 16 quorum queues, 39 bindings — and registers `mail.url.inspection_requested.v1`, `mail.url.inspection_completed.v1`, `mail.url.clicked.v1`. Full reference: [messaging-topology.md](messaging-topology.md). Declared as data in `packages/event-contracts/src/topology.ts` (`TOPOLOGY_VERSION = 4`; v4 makes `scan.attachment` a work queue (consumer `email-worker.scan.attachment`, user `eg-scanner`) and registers `mail.attachment.sandbox_requested.v1`, bindings unchanged; v2 added two event types and five bindings; v3 makes `scan.spam` a work queue and removes the two event bindings of `scan.auth` (35 bindings); the six exchanges, fourteen queues and their policies are those of v1) with the event registry in `registry.ts`, generated into `infrastructure/rabbitmq/definitions.json` and imported into the vhost `email-gateway` by `infrastructure/rabbitmq/init.mjs` (Compose `rabbitmq-init`, `pnpm rabbitmq:init`). IMPLEMENTED and tested (`topology.test.ts`, `registry.test.ts`; the committed definitions are compared with the generated ones). Services never declare: their users have `configure = ^$`.

| Exchange (topic, durable) | Events                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `email.ingestion`         | `mail.message.received.v1`, `mail.message.parsed.v1`, `mail.message.parse_failed.v1`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `email.security`          | `mail.message.scanned.v1`, `mail.message.rescan_requested.v1` (step 3.3), `mail.attachment.sandbox_requested.v1` (step 4.2; consumed by the sandbox in 4.6), `mail.url.inspection_requested.v1`, `mail.url.inspection_completed.v1`, `mail.url.clicked.v1` (step 4.3), `mail.message.scan_stage_ready.v1` (step 4.4), `mail.message.dlp_incident.v1` (step 4.5), `mail.sandbox.task_requested.v1`, `mail.sandbox.task_completed.v1`, `mail.attachment.sandbox_completed.v1` (step 4.6) |

Lossy by design: the relay never stalls on the archive, and the policy reconciler recaptures every dropped request from `policy_execution_logs` (§31) |

| Rule            | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Routing keys    | Events `org.<organizationId>.<eventType>`, bindings `org.*.<eventType>`; internal hops `retry.<queue>.org.<id>.<type>`, `delay.email.delivery.pending.org.<id>.<type>`, `retry.email.delivery.pending.expired`, `dead-letter.<queue>[.org.<id>.<type>]` (never in the firehose)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Publishing      | Only the event-relay publishes events (from `message_outbox`, confirm channel, `mandatory`, persistent), and marks a row published only after the broker confirmed it (step 3.2 WP3, ADR-0023). Nothing publishes yet                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Policies        | Per queue `eg.<queue>`: work `delivery-limit 20`, dead-letter to `email.events` (`at-least-once`), `reject-publish`, `max-length 1000000`; dormant `max-length 10000` `drop-head`; delay dead-letters to `email.delivery`; dead-letter `reject-publish`. `x-queue-type` is the only queue argument                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Consuming       | Manual acks after the handler's transaction commits, prefetch per consumer (`MIME_CONCURRENCY`, `DELIVERY_APPROVAL_PREFETCH`), retry with exponential backoff (hold or delay queue) and dead-lettering in the client library (step 3.2 WP2). Broker `consumer_timeout` 30 min                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Redelivery      | At-least-once. Every consumer wraps its effects in `processOnce(prisma, '<consumer>', eventId, handler)`; the reconciler re-drives events without a ledger row (ADR-0023)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Connection loss | Reconnect with backoff, consumers re-subscribe, the process stays up (step 3.2 WP2). Until WP4 the running services still use the crash-only `RabbitMqModule` on the legacy vhost `/`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Least privilege | Role users `eg-relay`, `eg-email-worker`, `eg-scanner` (step 4.1: reads `scan.spam`, publishes only its retry/dead-letter hops), `eg-delivery-worker`, `eg-policy-engine` and `eg-notification` (step 5.2: each reads only its own work queue(s) and publishes only its retry/dead-letter hops; since v10 `eg-policy-engine` reads `email.policy.evaluate` and `email.quarantine`), `eg-webhook` (step 6.2: reads `email.webhooks`, writes only its retry/dead-letter hops on `email.events`), `eg-ops` (replay also to `email.security` for `scan.spam`) (no tags, `configure ^$`); `init.mjs` prunes bindings on topology objects that the definitions do not declare; each service reads only its own URL key (§ users in [messaging-topology.md](messaging-topology.md#vhost-users-and-permissions-q10)) |
| Bodies          | Never on the bus: payloads carry ids, S3 keys, hashes, codes and counts only; content guard (WP2), broker `max_message_size` 1 MiB                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

## 5. Event envelope

`eventEnvelopeSchema` (zod), IMPLEMENTED:

| Field            | Type                                    | Contract                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `eventId`        | UUID (`z.uuid()`); producers use UUIDv7 | Idempotency key: consumers record `(consumer, eventId)` in `message_idempotency`                                                            |
| `eventType`      | `^[a-z]+(\.[a-z_]+)+\.v\d+$`            | Versioned, also the routing key, e.g. `mail.message.received.v1`                                                                            |
| `occurredAt`     | ISO 8601 date-time                      | When the state change happened                                                                                                              |
| `correlationId`  | string, 8–128 chars                     | Carried from the SMTP session or HTTP request (`x-correlation-id`) into every log line                                                      |
| `organizationId` | decimal string `^[1-9][0-9]{0,19}$`     | The tenant's `organizations.id` (BIGINT UNSIGNED) as a canonical decimal string. Set by the producer from trusted context, never from input |
| `payload`        | per event type                          | Parsed with the event's own schema by `parseEvent(raw, schema)`; any mismatch throws                                                        |

Ids inside the platform (events, S3 keys, logs) are decimal strings because JSON numbers cannot represent 64-bit integers. APIs expose `public_id` (UUIDv7) instead (ADR-0014).

## 6. Event payloads

Every message event carries `messageId` (the registry id, `message_identities.id`, decimal string) and `receivedAt` (the partition key of every message table), so a consumer reaches each partitioned table with `WHERE message_id = ? AND received_at = ?` and prunes to one partition.

| Event type                                  | Producer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Consumers (queue)                                                                                                                                      | Payload fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `mail.message.received.v1`                  | ingestion in `smtp-adapter` (3.1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `email-worker`                                                                                                                                         | `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction` (`inbound`, `outbound`, `internal`), `emlS3Key` (`^org/[1-9][0-9]{0,19}/`), `sizeBytes` (int ≥ 0), `envelopeFrom` (≤ 320), `recipientCount` (int ≥ 1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `mail.message.scanned.v1`                   | `email-worker` scan role (4.1), in the round's completion transaction                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `email.policy.evaluate` (policy-engine, step 5.2); the firehose                                                                                        | Redefined in 4.1 (the placeholder was never produced): `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction`, `emlS3Key`, `lifecycleRound`, `scanId`, `scanKind` (`initial` \| `rescan`), `outcome` (`scanned` \| `scan_failed`), `verdict` (seven verdicts), `engines` (1–16 `{ engine, required, outcome, attempts }`), `reasonCodes` (≤ 32), `completedAt`. Ids, codes and counts only (no address, value, masked sample or name; content guard)                                                                                                                                                                                       |

Nothing secret (reaches the firehose)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

No verdict, no names, no paths, no content                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

Schemas are IMPLEMENTED and tested (`events.test.ts`: valid events, UUID ids rejected, leading zeros rejected, non-prefixed S3 keys rejected, unversioned types rejected). `received.v1` and `scanned.v1` have producers and consumers (3.1/3.2, 4.1), `scan_stage_ready.v1` since 4.4 (producer: the scan completion transaction; consumer: the `bec` engine on `scan.bec`); since step 5.2 `mail.policy.decided.v1`, `mail.message.archive_requested.v1` and `mail.policy.notification_requested.v1` have a producer (the policy-engine) and `notification_requested.v1` a consumer (the notification-worker); `decided.v1` (5.3) and `archive_requested.v1` (8.1) wait in dormant queues.

**Domain events (step 2.1).**

| Event type                 | Producer                                      | Consumers (planned)                                                          | Payload fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `domain.status.changed.v1` | `api` (domains module), written to the outbox | smtp-adapter tenant-resolution cache invalidation (2.2), notifications (6.2) | `domainId` (decimal string), `domainPublicId` (UUID), `name` (ASCII, ≤ 253), `previousStatus` (string or `null`), `status`, `reason` (≤ 512 or `null`: `claimed_by_another_organization`, `verification_expired`, a suspension reason), `changedAt` (ISO date-time) |

Schema `domainStatusChangedV1` and `EventTypes.DomainStatusChanged` in `packages/event-contracts` (IMPLEMENTED, tested). One event per **status transition** (verified, failed by expiry or a lost claim, verification restarted, activated, suspended, resumed, deleted), never per DNS check, written to `message_outbox` in the transaction of the transition (`aggregate_type = 'domain'`, `aggregate_id` = decimal domain id, `organization_id` = the owning organization, `correlation_id` from the HTTP request or `domain-job:<tick uuid>` for background checks). A transition of another organization's pending claim (first to verify wins) is emitted for that organization. **Transport (step 3.2):** the event-relay (WP3, not built yet) publishes it to `email.events` with `org.<id>.domain.status.changed.v1`; topology v1 binds it only to the `email.analytics` firehose (the smtp-adapter cache invalidation stays an outbox tail). Until the relay runs, rows are written but not published and are kept by the partition drop guard; consumers must treat the event as at-least-once and idempotent by `eventId`.

**Delivery and routing events (step 2.3, [design R2, R5, R9](../plan/steps/05-routing.md)).** Schemas in `packages/event-contracts` (IMPLEMENTED, tested in `events.test.ts`), written to `message_outbox` in the transaction of their state change. Ids are decimal strings, datetimes ISO 8601; **no addresses and no content** in any payload.

| Event type                        | Producer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Consumers                                                                                                                           | Payload fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| --------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `mail.delivery.approved.v1`       | policy-engine (`source = policy`, step 5.2; `source = quarantine_release` by the 5.3 release executor); the step 3.3 api operator release no longer produces it (5.3 Q30: `409 quarantine_workflow_required`); the dev pass-through (`dev_passthrough`) was removed in 5.2 | delivery-worker planner (consumer `delivery-worker.planner`; outbox poller until step 3.2 WP4 moves it to `email.delivery.pending`) | §18                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `mail.delivery.status_changed.v1` | the shared recorder (`recordDeliverySignal`): delivery-worker (planner, reinjector, status tailer, reconciler), smtp-adapter (bounce notices)                                                                                                                              | none yet (firehose `email.analytics` once the relay runs, step 3.2; webhooks and notifications 6.2)                                 | `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `deliveryId`, `recipientId`, `rcptSeq`, `deliverySeq`, `direction`, `previousStatus` (`null` for a new row), `status` (`pending`, `queued`, `deferred`, `delivered`, `bounced`, `expired`, `failed`, `suppressed`, `unknown`, `in_progress`), `occurredAt`, `attemptCount`, optional `smtpCode`, `enhancedStatus`, `errorClass`, `queueId`. `aggregate_type = 'message'`, `correlation_id = 'delivery:<deliveryId>'`; one row per **applied** transition only |
| `domain.routing.changed.v1`       | api (`PUT`/`DELETE /domains/{id}/destinations`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | smtp-adapter outbox-tail invalidator (deletes the domain's route key and the TLS keys of the hosts)                                 | `domainId`, `domainPublicId`, `name`, `hosts` (old and new, ≤ 20), `changedAt`; `aggregate_type = 'domain'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

**MIME ingestion events (step 3.1, [design M14](../plan/steps/06-mime-ingestion.md#m14-events)).** Schemas `messageParsedV1` and `messageParseFailedV1` with `EventTypes.MessageParsed` and `EventTypes.MessageParseFailed` in `packages/event-contracts` (IMPLEMENTED, tested in `events.test.ts`), plus the code lists `MIME_LIMIT_NAMES`, `MESSAGE_PARSE_STATUSES` and `PARSE_FAILURE_REASONS`. Written by email-worker through `message_outbox` in the transaction that finalizes `email_message_parses` (`aggregate_type = 'message'`, `aggregate_id` = decimal message id, `correlation_id` copied from the `mail.message.received.v1` event, envelope `organizationId` = the message's organization). **Ids, keys, hashes, codes and counts only: never an address, the subject, a filename or a header value.** Consumers read `email_attachments` and `email_message_parses` from the writer. Topology v1 (§4) routes `parsed.v1` to the dormant `scan.*` queues and `parse_failed.v1` to the dormant `email.policy.evaluate` (and both to the firehose); the relay that publishes them is step 3.2 WP3.

| Event type                     | Producer                      | Consumers (planned)                     | Payload fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------ | ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `mail.message.parsed.v1`       | email-worker (MIME ingestion) | scanning (4.x), message lifecycle (3.3) | `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction`, `emlS3Key` (org-prefixed), `parseStatus` (`parsed`, `parsed_with_defects`, `parse_limit_exceeded`), `parserVersion` (1–32), `limitsExceeded` (`MIME_LIMIT_NAMES`, ≤ 16, unique), `defects` (codes `^[a-z0-9_]{1,48}$`, ≤ 64), `textBody` / `htmlBody` (`{ s3Key` org-prefixed or `null` when not stored, `sha256` lower-case hex, `sizeBytes }` or `null` when absent), `attachmentCount`, `flaggedAttachmentCount` (≤ `attachmentCount`), `attachmentFlags` (codes, ≤ 64, unique: the union over attachments), `partCount`, `maxDepth`, `headerCount` (ints ≥ 0). Consistency: `parse_limit_exceeded` iff `limitsExceeded` is non-empty; `parsed_with_defects` has ≥ 1 defect; `parsed` has none |
| `mail.message.parse_failed.v1` | email-worker (MIME ingestion) | message lifecycle (3.3), policy (5.x)   | `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction`, `emlS3Key` (org-prefixed, or `null` when the stored key has no `org/<id>/` prefix at all: `eml_key_mismatch` only), `reason` (`eml_missing`, `eml_key_mismatch`, `integrity_mismatch`, `max_attempts_exceeded`, `parser_error`), `attempts` (int ≥ 1), `parserVersion`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**Message lifecycle events (step 3.3, [design L7](../plan/steps/08-message-lifecycle.md#l7-events-packagesevent-contracts-registry-topology-v2), [ADR-0025](adr/0025-message-lifecycle-state-machine.md)).** Schemas `messageStateChangedV1` and `messageRescanRequestedV1` with `EventTypes.MessageStateChanged` and `EventTypes.MessageRescanRequested`, plus `MESSAGE_STATES` / `messageStateSchema` / `MessageStateValue` (the fifteen states in rank order) and `ACTOR_ROLES` / `actorRoleSchema` / `ActorRoleValue`, in `packages/event-contracts` (IMPLEMENTED, tested in `events.test.ts` and `registry.test.ts`; the producers are step 3.3 WP2–WP4). Through `message_outbox` (`aggregate_type = 'message'`). No actor id, no address, no content.

| Event type                             | Exchange         | Producer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Consumers                                                                                                                                                                                    | Payload fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `mail.message.state_changed.v1`        | `email.events`   | the transition service of `@email-gateway/message-lifecycle`, for every **applied** transition except creation (envelope `eventId` = `email_message_status_history.state_event_id`, correlation from the request or `message:<id>`)                               | none yet (firehose `email.analytics`; webhooks 6.2)                                                                                                                                          | `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction`, `previousState` (`MESSAGE_STATES` or `null`), `state`, `version` (int ≥ 0, after the transition), `lifecycleRound` (0–65 535), `reasonCode` (`^[a-z0-9_]{1,64}$`), `actorRole` (`ACTOR_ROLES`), `actorType` (`system` \| `user` \| `api_key`), `actorService` (1–64), `causationEventId` (UUID or `null`), `changedAt` |
| `mail.message.rescan_requested.v1`     | `email.security` | api administrative rescan (`POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/rescan`, edge E19, role `operator`) and, since step 4.2, the email-worker rescan job runner (E19, role `scanner`, reason `signature_rescan`, A13), in the transaction that moves the message to `scanning` | `scan.spam` (Rspamd, 4.1), `scan.attachment` (attachment engine, 4.2) `scan.url` (URL engine, 4.3) and the dormant `scan.dlp` (4.5); consumers check `lifecycleRound` against the writer row | `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction`, `emlS3Key` (org-prefixed), `lifecycleRound` (1–65 535, the new round), `scanKind` (`rescan`), `requestedAt`, `requestedBy` (`user` \| `api_key` \| `system`; `system` = a signature-update job, added in step 4.2)                                                                                                     |
| `mail.attachment.sandbox_requested.v1` | `email.security` | email-worker attachment engine (step 4.2 A11), in the finalize transaction of the attachment engine's final row, one per `sandbox_submissions` row (≤ `ATTACHMENT_SANDBOX_MAX_PER_MESSAGE`)                                                                       | the dormant `scan.sandbox` (sandbox engine, 4.6); consumers re-check the part key against the envelope organization (`organizationIdOfKey`)                                                  | `submissionId`, `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `direction`, `scanId`, `lifecycleRound`, `attachment` (`partIndex`, `sha256`, `sizeBytes`, `detectedType` (A5 vocabulary), `objectKey` org-prefixed `org/<id>/part/…`), `attachmentVerdict` (`suspicious` \| `unknown`), `reasonCodes` (≤ 16), `requestedAt`. Never a filename or content                           |
| `mail.url.inspection_requested.v1`     | `email.security` | email-worker URL engine (step 4.3 U15), in the finalize transaction of the url final row, one per `url_dynamic_inspections` row (≤ `URL_DYNAMIC_MAX_PER_MESSAGE`)                                                                                                 | `scan.url.inspect` (url-browser-worker, stateless; lossy queue). **The only event that carries a URL**; the analytics consumer (not built) must drop or hash `url`                           | `inspectionId`, `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `scanId`, `lifecycleRound`, `urlSeq`, `url` (http(s) identity form, no userinfo, ≤ 2 048), `urlSha256`, `requestedAt`, `deadlineAt` (> `requestedAt`)                                                                                                                                                               |
| `mail.url.inspection_completed.v1`     | `email.security` | url-browser-worker by **direct publish with publisher confirms** (no database); the request is acked only after the confirm                                                                                                                                       | `scan.url.results` (email-worker results consumer; a result for an unknown inspection id or another organization is dropped, never creates a row)                                            | `inspectionId`, `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `urlSeq`, `urlSha256`, `status` (`completed` \| `failed` ⇔ `errorCode`), `workerVersion`, `browserVersion`, `finalUrlSha256`, `finalHost`, `navigations` (≤ 10 hosts/kinds/statuses), `signals` (counts, booleans, hosts, brand ids), `durationMs`, `completedAt`. No body, title or screenshot                     |
| `mail.url.clicked.v1`                  | `email.security` | click-service via the outbox, in the click event's transaction                                                                                                                                                                                                    | no queue (reaches only the `email.analytics` firehose through the exchange binding)                                                                                                          | `clickId` (= `url_click_events.public_id`), `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `urlSeq`, `decision`, `verdict` (null for `expired`/`not_found`), `reasonCodes` (≤ 16), `clickedAt`. No URL, no IP, no user                                                                                                                                                             |

- **`mail.message.received.v1` is the notification of `persisted`.** The SMTP commit creates the message in `persisted` with the history rows `NULL → received` and `received → persisted` (design L5.2) and writes no `state_changed` event for them: `received.v1` already announces the committed message.
- **`mail.delivery.approved.v1` gains an optional `lifecycleRound`** (int 0–65 535; absent = round 0; non-breaking). The api release writes it from step 3.3 on (and the dev pass-through did until its removal), and the policy-engine writes it since 5.2. The planner never plans an approval of another round than the message's (design L8.4).

**Planned events** (names fixed by their steps): quarantine release request and approval (5.3, implemented); the webhook fan-out (6.2) is implemented as a projection of the internal events into public webhook events (§30), with the two internal events above as its only new contracts. `mail.policy.decided.v1` carries one disposition for the whole message; step 5.2 decided against per-recipient dispositions (its design §7, §10: the strictest disposition across recipients applies and recipient exceptions need the quantifier `all`); per-recipient decisions would need a `v2`.

## 7. Security engine contract

Step 4.1 ([ADR-0026](adr/0026-security-scan-pipeline.md), supersedes ADR-0007's contract; [S4](../plan/steps/09-rspamd.md#s4-the-engine-registry), [S7](../plan/steps/09-rspamd.md#s7-verdict-model-and-the-rspamd-mapping)). IMPLEMENTED and unit-tested.

| Part                                           | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Verdicts (`@email-gateway/security-contracts`) | `SCAN_VERDICTS` = `clean`, `spam`, `suspicious`, `malicious`, `unknown`, `timeout`, `error` (lower-case everywhere); `ENGINE_OUTCOMES` adds `skipped`. `timeout`/`error` are failures, never definite; `VERDICT_SEVERITY` `clean < unknown < suspicious < spam < malicious`; `mostSevere()` returns `null` without a definite verdict (never invents `clean`). Reason codes `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z0-9_]+)+$`, ≤ 64 characters, ≤ 32 per result                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Engine catalog                                 | `ENGINE_CATALOG` (data): `{ name, stage, queue, requiredByDefault, tenantMayDisable, dependsOn?, mode?, description }`; step 4.6 X10: `mode: 'asynchronous'` marks an engine the round never waits for (excluded from the round aggregation; never required, no `dependsOn`, nobody depends on it, refused by `parseEnabledEngines`, `isAsynchronousEngine()`): the `sandbox` entry (stage `sandbox`, queue `scan.sandbox` = where its work is dispatched, not required, disableable by a tenant), enabled by `SANDBOX_ENABLED`, not `SCAN_ENGINES`; 4.1: `rspamd` (`antispam`, `scan.spam`, required, not disableable); 4.2: `attachment` (`attachment`, `scan.attachment`, required for every tenant and direction, not disableable; ClamAV and YARA are components of this one engine, ADR-0028). `ENGINE_NAMES` = `rspamd, attachment, url, bec, dlp, sandbox`; `SCAN_ENGINES` default `rspamd,attachment`. `parseEnabledEngines(SCAN_ENGINES)`, `requiredEngines(enabled, tenantDisabled)` (snapshotted into `email_scans.required_engines`) |
| Result                                         | `normalizedEngineResultSchema`: `engine`, `engineVersion`, `outcome` (the five definite verdicts only), `score`, `requiredScore`, `action`, `reasonCodes`, `signals` (≤ 200 `{ name, score, options ≤ 5 }`), optional `auth` (`authResultsSchema`: SPF, DKIM with ≤ 10 signatures, DMARC with policy, ARC), `durationMs`, `settingsVersion`, `mappingVersion`. Step 4.2: `EngineDefinite.result` may be `skipped` (rule A0 `attachment.none`); per-attachment `attachmentResultSchema` (strict: no filename, content or entry name; `clean` ⇔ `clean.no_findings` with a definite ClamAV `OK`) carried as `childRows` and stored in `attachment_scan_results`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Adapter (email-worker)                         | `ScanEngineAdapter.scan(ctx, signal) → { kind: 'definite', result, evidence? } \| { kind: 'failure', outcome: 'timeout' \| 'error', errorCode, retryable, engineFault? }`; `ctx` carries the trusted envelope from the writer row (client IP, HELO, client name, envelope sender, recipients, queue id), a streaming EML opener and the effective settings. Registered in `ENGINE_REGISTRY`; the orchestrator never names an engine                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Settings                                       | `rspamdPlatformSettingsSchema` (env `SCAN_RSPAMD_*`, read identically by the api and the worker through `rspamdPlatformSettingsFromEnv`), `rspamdTenantSettingsSchema` (strict), `resolveRspamdSettings(platform, override)` (scores inside the bounds and ordered, severities only raised), `settingsVersion()` = `<mapping>;p=<12 hex>;t=<row version \| none>`. Step 4.2: `attachmentPlatformSettingsSchema` (env `ATTACHMENT_*`, `attachmentPlatformSettingsFromEnv`), `attachmentTenantSettingsSchema` (strict; raise-only verdicts, tighten-only limits and type lists), `resolveAttachmentSettings`, `attachmentSettingsBounds`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

Rules: engines classify, policy decides (an engine never rejects, quarantines or delivers); an engine timeout, error, open breaker, missing EML, unparseable reply or skipped scan is never `clean`.

## 8. Engine wire protocols

| Engine             | Endpoint (Compose)                                                                 | Protocol                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Auth                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Rspamd             | `SCAN_RSPAMD_URL=http://rspamd:11333`, `RSPAMD_CONTROLLER_URL=http://rspamd:11334` | IMPLEMENTED 4.1 (`@email-gateway/rspamd`): `POST /checkv2`, the EML **streamed** (`Content-Length`), headers `IP`, `Helo`, `Hostname`, `From`, `Rcpt` (≤ 100), `Queue-Id`, `User` (outbound/internal only), `Settings-Id` (`eg_inbound`/`eg_outbound`), `Log-Tag` (`m<id>r<round>`); reply validated by `rspamdCheckReplySchema`, version from the `Server` header. Controller `/learnspam`, `/learnham`, `/fuzzyadd`, `/fuzzydel`, `/stat`                                                                                                                          | `/checkv2`: none, internal networks only. Controller: read password (`RSPAMD_CONTROLLER_PASSWORD`, worker `/stat`), enable password (`RSPAMD_CONTROLLER_ENABLE_PASSWORD`, api training only); stored hashed; loopback needs none (health check) |
| ClamAV             | `CLAMAV_HOST=clamav`, `CLAMAV_PORT=3310`                                           | IMPLEMENTED 4.2 (`@email-gateway/clamav`, A7): one TCP connection per scan with `zIDSESSION\0`, `zVERSION\0`, `zINSTREAM\0` + 4-byte big-endian length-prefixed chunks (`CLAMAV_CHUNK_BYTES`) + `[0,0,0,0]`, `zEND\0`; replies matched by request number (`<n>: ClamAV <engine>/<db>/<date>`, `<n>: stream: OK` / `<sig> FOUND` / `INSTREAM size limit exceeded. ERROR`); `zPING\0` for readiness; at most `CLAMAV_MAX_CONNECTIONS`, timeout `CLAMAV_TIMEOUT_MS`; a size-limit reply is not retryable, every other error is a retryable engine failure (never clean) | None; internal network only                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| YARA-X (inspector) | child processes of the email-worker (`ATTACHMENT_INSPECTOR_POOL_SIZE`)             | IMPLEMENTED 4.2 (A4): Node IPC with `serialization: advanced`; worker → child `{ id, op: "inspect", bytes, limits }`, child → worker `{ id, ok, result \| code }`; handshake `{ op: "ready", yaraXVersion, bundleId, ruleCount }` (the pool refuses a different bundle id). Rules: the platform bundle `apps/email-worker/yara` (bundle id `yara-<n>-<12 hex>`), compiled once per child; `yara_rules` (tenant rules) stays unused                                                                                                                                   | n/a (no credentials or environment in the child)                                                                                                                                                                                                |

## 9. HTTP health contract

Every Nest service exposes the same unversioned endpoints (`HealthController`). IMPLEMENTED and tested (`health.service.test.ts`, per-app e2e and integration tests).

| Endpoint            | Checks                                                            | Status codes                           |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| `GET /health/live`  | None: the process and event loop respond                          | `200` always                           |
| `GET /health/ready` | Every registered indicator, **2 s timeout each**, run in parallel | `200` when all are ok, `503` otherwise |

Response (`HealthReport`): `service`, `status` (`ok` or `error`), `version`, `uptimeSeconds`, `checks[]`. Each check (`DependencyHealth`): `name`, `status`, `latencyMs`, optional `error` (message), optional `details` (flat map of string, number, boolean or null).

| Service               | HTTP port | Readiness checks                                         |
| --------------------- | --------- | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| `api`                 | 3000      | `mysql`, `mysql-reader`, `redis`, `s3`                   |
| `smtp-adapter`        | 3010      | `mysql`, `redis`, `policy-listener`                      |
| `email-worker`        | 3020      | `mysql`, `rabbitmq`, `redis`, `s3`                       |
| `policy-engine`       | 3030      | `mysql`, `rabbitmq`, `redis`                             |
| `delivery-worker`     | 3040      | `mysql`, `rabbitmq`                                      |
| `notification-worker` | 3050      | `mysql`, `rabbitmq`                                      |
| `archive-worker`      | 3060      | `mysql`, `rabbitmq`, `s3`                                |
| `admin-portal`        | 3001      | `GET /api/health`: liveness only, independent of the API |

What each check does: `mysql` runs `SELECT 1` on the writer; `rabbitmq` passively declares `mail.events`; `redis` expects `PONG`; `s3` runs `HeadBucket` on the mail bucket; `policy-listener` checks that the TCP listener is accepting.

**`mysql-reader`** (api only) always resolves with `status: "ok"` and reports the read-replica state in `details`:

```json
{
  "name": "mysql-reader",
  "status": "ok",
  "latencyMs": 3,
```
