```json
  "details": {
    "mode": "writer-fallback",
    "lagSeconds": null,
    "maxLagSeconds": 10,
    "reason": "replication stopped"
  }
}
```

`mode` is `reader`, `writer-fallback` or `writer-only`; `reason` is present only in `writer-fallback`. Readiness does not fail on the replica by design, because the writer fallback keeps the api correct; alert on `details.mode != "reader"` instead (PLANNED 10.3). Details: [replication.md](../database/replication.md).

## 10. api ↔ admin-portal

| Aspect                               | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Status                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Dashboard health                     | The portal server (never the browser) calls `${API_INTERNAL_URL}/health/ready` with `cache: no-store` and a 2 s timeout, and shows `ok`, `degraded` or `unreachable`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | IMPLEMENTED (`platform-health.test.ts`, `services.test.ts`)                                                                                                                             |
| Credentials                          | The portal holds no data-store credentials; its only backend is the api. `API_INTERNAL_URL` is server-side only                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | IMPLEMENTED                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Business API                         | REST under `/api/v1` only (ADR-0015, ADR-0050; the probes `/health/*` and `/metrics` are internal, ADR-0053), OpenAPI at `/docs` outside production with the snapshot `docs/api/openapi.json` (249 operations, every tag described, `x-eg-*` extensions). Bare resources, collections `{ data, nextCursor }` (quarantine lists: `items`), strict request schemas (unknown fields `400 validation_failed`). Endpoints and conventions: [docs/api/README.md](../api/README.md)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | IMPLEMENTED 6.1 (`route-inventory.e2e.test.ts`, `public-boundary.e2e.test.ts`, `openapi.test.ts`, `route-security*.e2e.test.ts`, `conventions.e2e.test.ts`, the API integration suites) |
| Browser calls                        | CORS limited to `CORS_ORIGINS` with credentials                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | IMPLEMENTED (config)                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Authentication                       | First-party authentication (ADR-0016): email + Argon2id password, TOTP MFA, short-lived JWT bearer access tokens with rotating refresh tokens (JSON bodies; the portal BFF decides cookie handling in 7.x), `X-API-Key` for automation; tenant always from the principal; platform staff reach a tenant only through the audited override. External SSO (OIDC/SAML) and SCIM come later behind the `sso` feature                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | IMPLEMENTED 1.3 (`apps/api/integration/auth.test.ts`, tenant isolation suites); SSO PLANNED (later step)                                                                                |
| Pagination, idempotency, rate limits | Cursor pagination `?limit&cursor` → `{ data, nextCursor }`, keyset order, no totals, short pages under a scan budget; **v1** cursors (every 6.1 list) HMAC-signed and bound to the organization, resource, filters, sort and window (`400 cursor_invalid`), **v0** unsigned cursors kept on the 33 earlier lists (ADR-0051). `Idempotency-Key` on the 37 sensitive mutations (transactional or reservation interceptor; secret-bearing routes excluded; `API_IDEMPOTENCY_LEGACY_MODE` for the 22 legacy routes) (ADR-0052). Global limits per principal, per tenant (`organization_limits.api_requests_per_minute`, clamped) and per class (`sensitive`, `expensive`) in Redis, fail closed, `RateLimit-*` headers, `429 rate_limited` with `scope`; route limits (authentication per IP, quarantine, …) in addition | IMPLEMENTED 6.1 (pagination since 1.2; auth limits 1.3; per-tenant limits, v1 cursors and the idempotency list 6.1)                                                                     |
| Error shape                          | `{ statusCode, error, code, message, correlationId, requestId, ...details }` from one global `ApiExceptionFilter`; default codes per status (`validation_failed`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `not_found`/`route_not_found`, `conflict`, `rate_limited`, `internal_error`, `service_unavailable`, …), module codes unchanged; `message` never echoes input                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | IMPLEMENTED 6.1 (`api-exception.filter.test.ts`, `conventions.e2e.test.ts`)                                                                                                             |
| Request-Id and correlation           | `X-Request-Id` on every response (server-minted UUID, never from the client), also in the error body, every log line and `audit_logs.metadata.requestId`; `x-correlation-id` accepted when well formed, echoed and audited; `Cache-Control: no-store` on `/api/v1` unless a route sets its own                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | IMPLEMENTED 6.1 (`request-id.middleware.test.ts`, `conventions.e2e.test.ts`)                                                                                                            |
| Public / internal boundary           | Only `/api/v1/*` is public; the api Ingress routes only `Prefix /api/v1/`; `/metrics`, `/health/*`, `/docs` never routed (`/docs` not served in production); `/api/metrics`, `/api/health/*`, `/api/docs` are `404` (ADR-0053)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | IMPLEMENTED 6.1 (`public-boundary.e2e.test.ts`, `route-inventory.e2e.test.ts`; Ingress rendered, not applied to a cluster)                                                              |
| Identifiers                          | `public_id` (UUIDv7) for every exposed aggregate; numeric ids never leave the platform through the API; `bigint` is never serialized raw (explicit response mappers)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | IMPLEMENTED for organizations, users and API keys (1.2/1.3); later resources follow the same rule                                                                                       |

## 11. S3 key conventions

Buckets come from configuration: `S3_BUCKET_MAIL` (EML and attachment parts) and `S3_BUCKET_ARCHIVE` (archive copies). Locally MinIO (path-style, `S3_ENDPOINT`); in AWS the Terraform `mail-storage` module (SSE-KMS, versioning, TLS-only, public access blocked).

| Object                              | Bucket  | Key                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Writer                                                            | Status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Raw message (EML)                   | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/eml/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{objectRef}.eml` (step 2.2 D8)                                                                                                                                                    | ingestion (smtp-adapter)                                          | IMPLEMENTED (2.2; stack suite 1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Body or attachment part             | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/part/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{messageId}/{partIndex}.{text\|html\|att}` (step 3.1 M11)                                                                                                                        | email-worker (MIME ingestion)                                     | IMPLEMENTED 3.1; read (never written) by the attachment engine 4.2: archive entries are inspected in memory and never stored (A18)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Rewritten copy (step 4.3)           | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/rewrite/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{messageId}/r{lifecycleRound}-{uuidv7}.eml` (U11; date of `received_at`, attempt-unique suffix)                                                                               | email-worker URL engine                                           | IMPLEMENTED 4.3: `ContentType: message/rfc822`, `ContentDisposition: attachment`, tag `eg-object=rewrite` (lifecycle rule `eg-rewrite-expiry`, `URL_REWRITE_OBJECT_RETENTION_DAYS`, orphans of losing attempts included); named by `url_rewrites.object_key` with SHA-256 and size; read by the delivery-worker through an approval (§18). The original EML is never modified                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Policy copy (step 5.2)              | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/policy/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{messageId}/r{lifecycleRound}-{32 hex of sha256(baseKey \| canonical edits)}.eml` (P21; date of `received_at`; deterministic, a retry overwrites the same bytes)               | policy-engine                                                     | IMPLEMENTED 5.2, extended 5.3: the delivered object of an `allow` with TAG/WARN header edits (`X-EG-Policy-Tag`, `X-EG-Policy-Warning`, subject prefix), since 5.3 also with a WARN **body banner** and/or **URL rewriting after the scan** (pipeline base → URL rewrite → banner → headers; the canonical edits include `banner`/`urlRewrite` only when present, so 5.2 keys are unchanged; ADR-0049), and every **release copy** (step 5.3 Q13.6: key `…/r<roundAfter>-<32 hex of sha256(baseKey \| canonical edits \| releaseId)>.eml`, always written because it carries `X-EG-Quarantine-Release: <release public id>`; that header is stripped from incoming headers in every copy); `ContentType: message/rfc822`, `ContentDisposition: attachment`, tag `eg-object=policy-copy` set on the creating request, expired by the MinIO/S3 lifecycle rule `eg-policy-copy-expiry` after `POLICY_COPY_RETENTION_DAYS` (30) |
| Sandbox sample (step 4.6)           | sandbox | `samples/{yyyy}/{mm}/{taskId}.bin` (X12; month of staging, UTC; deterministic, an idempotent overwrite)                                                                                                                      | email-worker sandbox dispatcher                                   | PLANNED 4.6 WP3 (format and key builders IMPLEMENTED in `@email-gateway/sandbox`): `sandbox-sample/1` (`EGSBXS1` ‖ u16 header length ‖ `{ v, kid, wrappedKey }` ‖ IV ‖ tag ‖ AES-256-GCM ciphertext; data key wrapped with `SANDBOX_SAMPLE_KEY`, AAD `eg-sandbox-sample/v1\0<taskId>\0<sha256>`); read only by the sandbox-controller's MinIO user (`GetObject samples/*`); deleted after the result, lifecycle backstop `SANDBOX_SAMPLE_RETENTION_HOURS`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Sandbox artifact (step 4.6)         | sandbox | `artifacts/{yyyy}/{mm}/{taskId}/{runId}/{seq}-{kind}.bin` (X12)                                                                                                                                                              | sandbox-controller                                                | PLANNED 4.6 WP2 (format IMPLEMENTED): `sandbox-artifact/1` (`EGSBXA1` ‖ IV ‖ tag ‖ ciphertext; data key wrapped to the X25519 `SANDBOX_ARTIFACT_PUBLIC_KEY` via ECDH + HKDF-SHA256, `x1.<kid>.<ephemeralPub>.<iv>.<tag>.<ct>` stored only in `sandbox_artifacts`); written only by the controller's MinIO user (`PutObject artifacts/*`); crypto-shredded and deleted by the sweeper, lifecycle backstop `SANDBOX_ARTIFACT_RETENTION_DAYS + 1`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| DLP evidence (step 4.5)             | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/dlp-evidence/{yyyy}/{mm}/{scanId}-{uuidv7}.bin` (D16; date of `received_at`, attempt-unique suffix)                                                                                                    | email-worker DLP engine                                           | IMPLEMENTED 4.5: envelope-encrypted (`dlp-evidence/1`: magic `EGDLP1` ‖ IV ‖ tag ‖ AES-256-GCM ciphertext; the data key wrapped with `DLP_EVIDENCE_KEY` exists only in the `dlp_evidence` row); written **before** the finalize transaction (an uncommitted object is undecryptable; the losing attempt deletes it); read only by the api's audited evidence endpoint; crypto-shredded and deleted by the evidence sweeper or a purge; deleted with the organization                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Archived original (step 8.1)        | archive | `org/{organizationId}/archive/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{messagePublicId}/{captureId}/eml.bin` (§31; date of `received_at`, attempt-scoped capture id, `archive-object/1` ciphertext)                                                 | archive-worker capture role                                       | IMPLEMENTED 8.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Journal report, scan snapshot (8.1) | archive | `…/{captureId}/journal.bin`, `…/{captureId}/scan-r{round}-v{revision}.bin` (same prefix as the archived original)                                                                                                            | archive-worker capture role                                       | IMPLEMENTED 8.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| eDiscovery export (8.1)             | archive | `org/{organizationId}/exports/{yyyy}/{mm}/{exportPublicId}.bin` (month of creation; tag `eg-object=archive-export`, never locked)                                                                                            | archive-worker ediscovery role                                    | IMPLEMENTED 8.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Assistant report (step AI-R)        | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/assistant/report/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{reportPublicId}.html` (§35.3, ADR-0095; UTC date of generation; `text/html; charset=utf-8`, PutObject with `ChecksumSHA256`; deleted with the conversation content) | api assistant module (writes, deletes); notification-worker reads | PLANNED AI-R (WP3, WP4)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| DSN copy                            | mail    | `org/{organizationId}/dsn/...` (proposed; layout fixed in 2.3, column `bounce_events.dsn_object_key`)                                                                                                                        | delivery-worker                                                   | PLANNED 2.3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

**EML key (changed in step 2.2, D8):** the date is the UTC date of the ingestion attempt and `{objectRef}` is the first 32 hex characters of SHA-256(`{mtaId}|{queueId}|{direction}|{organizationId}`), so a same-day retry overwrites its own object instead of orphaning one; the message id does not exist before the transaction that follows the upload. The key is stored in `email_messages.eml_object_key` and carried in `mail.message.received.v1`; **consumers never derive it**. Orphans (upload succeeded, commit never did) are reconciled in step 3.2.

**Part key (step 3.1, M11):** deterministic. The date is the UTC date of the message's `received_at` (never "now"), `{organizationId}` and `{messageId}` come from the writer row, and `{partIndex}` is the node's index in `email_message_parses.structure`, so a retry, a redelivery or a concurrent worker writes the same key with the same bytes. `.text` and `.html` hold the transfer-decoded, UTF-8-converted text and HTML bodies, `.att` the decoded bytes of every other leaf. Filenames and header values never appear in a key or in object metadata; objects are stored with `ContentDisposition: attachment` (`ContentType` `text/plain; charset=utf-8` for `.text`, `application/octet-stream` otherwise). Keys are stored in `email_message_parses.{text,html}_body_object_key` and `email_attachments.object_key` (NULL when a size limit kept the part from being stored).

Rules: `{organizationId}` is a decimal id. Every key starts with `org/{organizationId}/` so per-tenant export, deletion and IAM conditions are prefix operations. The event schema enforces the **format** (`^org/[1-9][0-9]{0,19}/`); consumers must also check that the prefix **equals** the envelope's `organizationId` before reading (PLANNED 3.x). MySQL stores keys (`*_object_key VARCHAR(512)`), sizes and SHA-256 hashes, never content.

## 12. Database connections

| Variable                 | Account                    | Grants                                                        | Used by                             |
| ------------------------ | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| `DATABASE_URL`           | `gateway_app` (writer)     | `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on the schema                | Every service                       |
| `DATABASE_READ_URL`      | `gateway_reader` (replica) | `SELECT` on the schema, `REPLICATION CLIENT` (lag probe only) | `api` only (`PrismaReadService`)    |
| `MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL` | `gateway_migrator`         | DDL on the schema                                             | `db-migrate`, partition maintenance |
| (replication)            | `gateway_repl`             | `REPLICATION SLAVE, BACKUP_ADMIN`                             | Replica I/O thread, CLONE           |

Data-plane services (`smtp-adapter`, `email-worker`, `policy-engine`, `delivery-worker`, `archive-worker`) use the writer only, so replica lag never affects a mail decision. `notification-worker` and `webhook-worker` (step 6.2) use the writer only (the fanout's projection reads, the delivery claims and the alert reads at send time). See [data ownership](data-ownership.md) and [replication.md](../database/replication.md).

## 13. Postfix → smtp-adapter socketmap

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2** (D2, D11). Postfix [socketmap](https://www.postfix.org/socketmap_table.5.html) tables on `:10041` (netstrings, request ≤ 10 000 bytes, malformed → close). Replies `OK <value>`, `NOTFOUND `, `TEMP <reason>` (DB error: Postfix defers, fail closed), `PERM Unknown map`.

| Map            | Used by                                     | Key → answer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `relay_domain` | `postfix` `relay_domains`                   | domain → `OK active` for an active, inbound-enabled tenant domain; else `NOTFOUND` (also `.parent`, `user@domain`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `transport`    | `postfix-outbound` `transport_maps`         | **full recipient address** (socketmap is a non-indexed table: Postfix sends `user@domain` once, never the bare domain) or a bare domain → **step 2.3 (R2): a next-hop list** of every enabled `inbound_destination` of the active tenant domain ordered by `(priority, id)`, `OK smtp:[h1]:25,[h2]:2525` (Postfix tries them in order: failover; at most `ROUTING_MAX_DESTINATIONS_PER_DOMAIN`). Each host is re-validated with `validateDestinationHost` (R3); invalid rows are skipped and logged; none left or any gateway host → `OK retry:4.4.1 Destination not configured` (on 10026 Postfix refuses the RCPT with `450 4.4.1`, which the worker records as `deferred`, class `no_destination`); non-tenant → `NOTFOUND` (MX resolution). **10027 mail never consults this map** (§15) |
| `tls_policy`   | `postfix-outbound` `smtp_tls_policy_maps`   | `[host]:port` of a tenant destination → `none` / `may` / `encrypt protocols=>=TLSv1.2` / `secure match=<tls_verify_names or nexthop> protocols=>=TLSv1.2` (strictest of the rows sharing it; the api keeps the rows of different organizations on one next hop in agreement: `destination_tls_conflict`, `host_reserved_for_provider`); else `NOTFOUND`. Postfix looks the policy up **per next hop** of a list (verified, [mail-routing.md](../deployment/mail-routing.md) V2). Provider presets (`mail_destination_presets`): Microsoft 365 `nexthop:mail.protection.outlook.com:.mail.protection.outlook.com`, Google Workspace `nexthop:mx.google.com:.aspmx.l.google.com` (verified against test-CA certificates with the providers' SAN sets, V3)                                      |
| `relay_client` | `postfix-outbound` 25 `check_client_access` | client IP inside an active `source_ip` connector network → `OK OK` (access action as value); else `NOTFOUND`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `relay_ccert`  | `postfix-outbound` 25 `check_ccert_access`  | SHA-256 certificate fingerprint of an active `client_certificate` connector → `OK OK`; else `NOTFOUND` (works with self-signed client certificates; verified by the stack suites)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

Note: `smtpd` caches address resolutions per process, so a relay-domain answer can outlive a domain change in one long-lived `smtpd`; the `inbound` policy re-checks the domain on every RCPT (D4 step 2).

## 14. Postfix → smtp-adapter SASL

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2** (D10). `postfix-outbound` 587 uses `smtpd_sasl_type = dovecot`, `smtpd_sasl_path = inet:${SMTP_ADAPTER_HOST}:10042`, `smtpd_tls_auth_only = yes`. The adapter implements the server side of the Dovecot auth protocol 1.2: handshake (`VERSION 1 2`, `MECH PLAIN`, `MECH LOGIN`, `SPID`, `CUID`, `COOKIE`, `DONE`), `AUTH` with `secured` required, PLAIN (authzid empty or equal) and LOGIN. Success `OK <id> user=<username>`; wrong or unknown credentials, disabled connector or inactive organization → `FAIL <id> user=…` after `SMTP_AUTH_FAILURE_DELAY_MS` (SMTP `535 5.7.8`). **Temporary failures** (DB/Redis error, more than `SMTP_AUTH_MAX_FAILURES_PER_IP` failures per window) **close the connection without a reply**, which Postfix answers with `454 4.7.0 Temporary authentication failure` — Postfix 3.7 turns any `FAIL` line, including `FAIL … temp`, into a permanent 535 (deviation from D10 found by the stack tests). Secrets are Argon2id PHC hashes (`smtp_connectors.secret_hash`); successful verifications are cached in process memory keyed by an HMAC that includes the connector's `updated_at`.

## 15. Reinjection (delivery-worker → postfix-outbound)

**Listeners IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2; the caller (delivery-worker) IMPLEMENTED in step 2.3** ([design R4, R8](../plan/steps/05-routing.md), ADR-0021 amends ADR-0019 decision 2: the worker is the one internal SMTP client). Only clients in `POSTFIX_REINJECT_NETWORKS` (production: the delivery-worker subnet); never published to the host or the Internet (locally and in CI the test-only `reinject-forwarder` lets a worker on the host reach them).

| Port    | For                                                               | Policy context      | Rules                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `10026` | approved **inbound and internal** mail → the tenant's own servers | `reinject-inbound`  | recipient must be an active tenant domain (never the bounce-notice domain); routed by the `transport`/`tls_policy` maps (next-hop list, §13); `RCPT … NOTIFY=NEVER` for inbound (no backscatter to external senders), `NOTIFY=FAILURE,DELAY` for internal (DSN to the authenticated sender)                                                                                                                                                       |
| `10027` | approved **outbound** mail → the Internet                         | `reinject-outbound` | sender domain must be an active, outbound-enabled tenant domain with a published signing key; DKIM-signed by `rspamd-outbound`; `NOTIFY=FAILURE,DELAY`; **`-o content_filter=smtp:`** (step 2.3, R4): the empty next hop means the recipient domain, so Postfix does its own MX lookup and **never consults `transport_maps`** — a recipient in another tenant's domain is reached through that tenant's public MX, never its destination servers |

Session (step 2.3, verified on Postfix 3.7, [mail-routing.md](../deployment/mail-routing.md#reinjection-session-contract-r8-verified)):

1. `EHLO ${DELIVERY_REINJECT_HELO}` (`delivery.gateway.local`, organization-neutral); `XFORWARD` must be advertised, else a configuration error (retry).
2. `XFORWARD NAME=… ADDR=… HELO=… PROTO=ESMTP` with the message's original client (`[UNAVAILABLE]` when unknown), then **`XFORWARD IDENT=<token> SOURCE=REMOTE`**. **Contract change (ADR-0021): `IDENT` is the reinjection token `dw1-` + the 32 hex digits of `delivery_reinjections.public_id`**, no longer the original queue id; Postfix logs it as `orig_queue_id=` on the `client=` line, which binds its queue id to the reinjection even if the worker crashes before reading the `250` (§19). The forwarded `ADDR` does not change `permit_mynetworks`. Deviation (WP2): Postfix's `Received:` of this hop names the real peer, not the XFORWARD client; with `smtpd_peername_lookup=no` it reads `from delivery.gateway.local (unknown [<worker ip>]) by out.gateway.local`.
3. `MAIL FROM:<original envelope sender> SIZE=<n> RET=HDRS ENVID=<token>` (+ `SMTPUTF8` for non-ASCII addresses). `ENVID` returns as `Original-Envelope-Id:` in DSNs and postmaster copies (second matching key of the bounce handler, §3).
4. `RCPT TO:<a> NOTIFY=<per direction> ORCPT=rfc822;<xtext a>` per recipient. `2xx` accepted; `4xx` → that recipient moves to a new reinjection with the worker backoff (`deferred`, `reinjection_4xx`, or `no_destination` for `Destination not configured`); `5xx` → `failed`, `reinjection_refused`.
5. `DATA`: one `X-Gateway-Trace` (§16) + the stored EML **unchanged** (streamed from S3; LF → CRLF, dot-stuffing). `250 … queued as <QID>` → `queued` (never `delivered`), `outbound_queue_id = QID`. A timeout after the final dot is ambiguous and left to the reconciler.

Postmaster copies of the resulting DSNs reach the bounce handler (§3).

## 16. Gateway trace header

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2** (D14; `buildGatewayTrace` / `countGatewayPasses` in `@email-gateway/common`). `X-Gateway-Trace: v=1; g=<GATEWAY_TRACE_ID>; d=<in|out>; t=<unix seconds>; s=<sig>`, `sig` = base64url (no padding) of the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA256(`GATEWAY_TRACE_SECRET`, `v1|<g>|<d>|<t>|<organizationId>`). It names neither the tenant nor an internal host. Ingestion counts valid traces for the same gateway, direction and organization; `≥ SMTP_LOOP_MAX_PASSES` → `554 5.4.6` for that organization's recipients. Forged traces and other tenants' or the other direction's traces do not count (stack suite 11).

**Step 2.3:** the delivery-worker prepends exactly one trace on **both** reinjection listeners: `d=in` on 10026 (inbound and internal), `d=out` on 10027. The 10026 trace is what stops a loop between tenants' routes at the second arrival for the same organization (`routing-loops.test.ts`). Before connecting, the worker also evaluates hops (`evaluateHops`): `Received:` count ≥ `ROUTING_MAX_RECEIVED_HEADERS` (25) or valid traces of this organization in **both** directions ≥ `ROUTING_MAX_GATEWAY_PASSES` (4) → `failed`, `loop_detected`, nothing sent.

## 17. DKIM key store (redis-dkim)

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.2** (D12, ADR-0020). The api's `DkimSigningPublisher` (every `DKIM_SIGNING_SYNC_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, one replica per tick, and on demand with `pnpm --filter @email-gateway/api dkim:sync`) decrypts the active RSA-2048 key of every active, outbound-enabled domain of an active organization (`SecretBox`, AAD `dkim:<domain public id>:<selector>`) and reconciles two hashes in `MULTI/EXEC`: `dkim_selectors` (`<domain>` → `<selector>`) and `dkim_keys` (`<selector>.<domain>` → PKCS#8 PEM). `rspamd-outbound` reads them as `dkim-reader` (`selector_prefix`/`key_prefix`), smtp-adapter probes `dkim_selectors` as `dkim-probe` (never keys). redis-dkim has no persistence; after a restart 10027 defers until the next sync.

## 18. Delivery approval (`mail.delivery.approved.v1`)

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.3** ([design R5, R6, R7](../plan/steps/05-routing.md)); the producers are the policy-engine (step 5.2, `source = policy`) and, since step 5.3, the policy-engine's **quarantine release executor** (`source = quarantine_release`; the step 3.3 api operator release no longer produces approvals: it answers `409 quarantine_workflow_required`, Q30). The trigger of delivery: the approved subset of a message's recipients, written through `message_outbox` (`aggregate_type = 'message'`, `aggregate_id` = decimal message id), envelope `organizationId` = the message's organization, envelope `eventId` = the approval id.

| Field            | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `messageId`      | registry id (decimal string)                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `receivedAt`     | partition key (ISO datetime)                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `deliverySeq`    | `0` = initial delivery; releases use `1, 2, …`; `(recipientId, deliverySeq)` is unique per delivery                                                                                              |
| `source`         | `policy` \| `quarantine_release` \| `dev_passthrough`                                                                                                                                            |
| `emlS3Key`       | the object to deliver (`org/<organizationId>/…`; the worker refuses another organization's prefix): the round's rewritten copy when one exists, else the original EML (rule below, step 4.3 U20) |
| `recipients`     | 1–1 000 `{ recipientId, rcptSeq }`, unique ids; the worker loads them from the writer and skips foreign or unknown ids                                                                           |
| `approvedAt`     | ISO datetime                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `lifecycleRound` | optional, int 0–65 535 (absent = 0): the lifecycle round the approval belongs to (step 3.3 L7, L8.4); a message of another round is not planned                                                  |

- **Transport:** behind the `ApprovalFeed` interface. Step 2.3 = an **outbox poller** (cursor `outbox:delivery-worker.planner` in `stream_cursors`, advanced only past rows older than `DELIVERY_OUTBOX_SAFETY_LAG_MS`, `processOnce` per row, single active poller per consumer with `GET_LOCK`). Step 3.2 (designed, WP4 not built yet) replaces it with a consumer of the queue `email.delivery.pending` (§4; routing key `org.<id>.mail.delivery.approved.v1` on `email.delivery`, prefetch `DELIVERY_APPROVAL_PREFETCH`, retry through the delay queue `email.delivery.retry`), with the same consumer name so existing ledger rows absorb the relay's backlog. The poller never touches `published_at`; the event-relay sets it (ADR-0023).
- **Quarantine release approvals (step 5.3, [Q13.2, Q13.5, Q13.6](../plan/steps/17-quarantine.md#q13-release-with-re-validation-adr-0047), [ADR-0047](adr/0047-quarantine-release-revalidation-and-approvals.md)).** The release executor (policy-engine `quarantine` role, consumer of `email.quarantine`) writes the approval of a released entry in the same writer transaction as the authoritative re-validation, the lifecycle edge for the message state (E14 / E15 / E28 with a new round, or no transition for `approved` / `delivery_pending` / `delivery_retry`, whose current round the approval carries), the recipients `quarantined → pending`, the entries `released` and the release log `dispatched`: `source = quarantine_release`, `deliverySeq` = highest + 1, `lifecycleRound` = the round after the edge, chunks ≤ 1 000, `emlS3Key` = the **release copy** (§11). **Delivered object of a release (amends U20 below):** the base is the latest _re-validated_ scan round's `url_rewrites` copy (`status = rewritten`, echo-checked) if its object still exists (HEAD), else the original EML — never another round's copy; the release edits (`releaseDeliveryEdits`: the TAG/WARN/URL_REWRITE edits the current result would apply if allowed) plus `X-EG-Quarantine-Release` are applied on top, so a release always delivers a policy copy; a copy refusal (size, header block) delivers the base and records the edits. Split messages (step 5.3 Q5): the policy-engine approves only the `pending` (allowed) recipients of a split round; held recipients are approved later by their releases.
- **Policy approvals (step 5.2, [P13, P14, P20, P21](../plan/steps/16-policy-engine.md#p13-where-policy-runs-adr-0044-the-policy-engine-consumer), [ADR-0044](adr/0044-policy-evaluation-placement-and-delivery-gating.md)).** The policy-engine writes the approvals of an `allow` disposition in its evaluation transaction: `source = policy`, `deliverySeq` = 0 for a first delivery (else the highest + 1), the round, chunks of ≤ 1000 `pending` recipients, `emlS3Key` = the round's rewritten copy (rule below) or the original, or — with TAG/WARN header edits — the **policy copy** `org/<org>/policy/…` (§11, §28). Every other disposition writes no approval. The planner labels `delivery_approvals_total{source, object}` with `object` = `rewritten` / `policy_copy` / `original` by key prefix. **The development pass-through and the four bullets after this one are history: removed in step 5.2 (ADR-0044 decision 3; E27 retired); `DELIVERY_DEV_PASSTHROUGH_ENABLED=true` is refused at startup in every environment; its holds are now the policy floor and the platform default (§28).**
- **Development-only pass-through (removed in 5.2)** (`DELIVERY_DEV_PASSTHROUGH_ENABLED=true`, refused at startup unless `APP_ENV` is `local` or `ci`): turns each `mail.message.received.v1` into an approval with `source = dev_passthrough` for every `pending` recipient, plus an `email_events` row `delivery.approved`. It delivers mail **without scanning or policy** and is never production behaviour.
- **BEC results (step 4.4 [B17](../plan/steps/12-phishing-bec.md#b17-development-pass-through-and-bec-verdicts); binding for policy 5.2).** The development pass-through does not approve a `scanned` round whose `scanned.v1.engines` entry for `bec` has outcome `suspicious` or `malicious` **and whose `bec_scan_results.deterministic_verdict` (the round's `scanId`) is `suspicious` or `malicious`**, or whose result row is missing (fail-safe) (ledger row only, `delivery_approvals_skipped_total{reason="bec_verdict"}`, the message stays `scanned` until policy or the `scanned` lifecycle deadline). The check runs after the `scan_failed` and `malicious` checks, so a bec `malicious` (which makes the round `malicious`) keeps the reason `malicious_verdict`; a `suspicious` URL or Rspamd verdict is still approved. **Policy (5.2) must treat the `bec` engine's result explicitly**: it reads the engine's outcome from `scanned.v1.engines` (and, for explanations, the `bec_scan_results` row of the round's `scanId`, §24) and decides per tenant policy; it must never derive a BEC decision from the round verdict alone, and a missing or failed `bec` final (`scan_failed`, `unknown`) is never treated as clean. **An LLM never alone holds, quarantines, rejects or releases mail ([ADR-0034](adr/0034-bec-ai-layer-invariant.md) amendment):** a bec `suspicious` whose deterministic verdict is `clean`/`unknown` (raised by the AI layer only: `bec.ai.flagged`, `ai[].effect = raised_to_suspicious`) may be tagged or warned on but is decided as the deterministic verdict; the pass-through approves it and counts `delivery_bec_ai_only_not_held_total`. `scanned.v1` itself is unchanged.
- **DLP actions (step 4.5 [D13](../plan/steps/13-dlp.md#d13-actions-lifecycle-binding-and-the-development-pass-through), [ADR-0037](adr/0037-dlp-policy-actions-and-lifecycle-binding.md); binding for policy 5.2 and quarantine 5.3).** After the `scan_failed`, `malicious` and BEC checks (their reasons unchanged), when the `scanned.v1.engines` entry for `dlp` has a definite outcome other than `skipped`, the pass-through reads the round's `dlp_scan_results` row (writer, inside its ledger transaction, echo-checked on organization, message, `received_at`, `scan_id` and round; a shifted read throws → redelivery, `delivery_inconsistent_reads_total{read="dlp_result"}`): `allow`/`alert` → approved (`alert` counted in `delivery_dlp_alerts_approved_total`); `require_approval`/`quarantine`/`block` → **not approved** (ledger row only, `delivery_approvals_skipped_total{reason="dlp_action"}`, `delivery_dlp_holds_total{action}`; the message stays `scanned` until policy or the `scanned` deadline); a **missing row → held** (fail-safe, `delivery_dlp_holds_total{action="missing"}`). `scanned.v1` is unchanged. **Policy (5.2) and quarantine (5.3) must apply the round's DLP action and may only be stricter, never weaker:** `require_approval` → E12 `held_for_review`, `quarantine` → E11, `block` → E13 with a non-delivery notice to the internal sender, `alert` → deliver without suppressing the incident; a missing row for a definite non-skipped dlp final is a `block`-equivalent hold; after a rescan the new round's row decides (§25).
- **Risk recommendation (step 5.1 [R17](../plan/steps/15-risk-scoring.md#r17-the-development-pass-through-localci-only), [ADR-0042](adr/0042-risk-scoring-placement-and-policy-handoff.md) decision 6).** After the `scan_failed`, `malicious`, BEC and DLP checks (their reasons unchanged), with `DELIVERY_DEV_PASSTHROUGH_RISK_HOLD=true` (default), the pass-through reads the round's revision-0 `message_risk_scores` row in its ledger transaction (writer, echo-checked; a shifted read → `delivery_inconsistent_reads_total{read="risk_score"}`, redelivered): `require_approval`/`quarantine`/`block`, a hard block or an error row → **not approved** (`delivery_approvals_skipped_total{reason="risk_recommendation"}`, `delivery_risk_holds_total{action}`); a missing row → not approved (`reason="risk_missing"`); `deliver`/`tag` → approved. It can only hold more than 4.6, never less. Binding for 5.2/5.3: [§27](#27-risk-scoring-step-51).
- **Idempotency:** a duplicate approval hits the `processOnce` ledger, the delivery unique key and the reinjection idempotency key `sha256(v1|messageId|deliverySeq|listener|recipientSetSha256)`.
- **Which object an approval delivers (step 4.3 [U11 "Delivery", U20](../plan/steps/11-url-protection.md#u11-url-rewriting-location-exclusions-signed-mail-mime-safety); binding for every approval producer: the policy-engine since 5.2 (`apps/policy-engine/src/delivery/delivery-object.ts`, moved from the removed pass-through) and the 5.3 quarantine release executor, which reuses the copy of the round it re-validated as described in the bullet above).** The producer sets `emlS3Key` to `url_rewrites.object_key` when a row with `status = rewritten` exists for the message, its organization, `received_at`, the approved `lifecycleRound` and that round's scan; otherwise to `email_messages.eml_object_key`. The read happens on the writer inside the producer's own transaction and is **echo-checked** (message, organization, `received_at`, round, scan id and status as asked; a shifted read is retryable, never acted on, step 4.2 §6); the key must start with `org/<organizationId>/rewrite/` and contain no `.`/`..`/empty segment, else the original is delivered and a warning logged. A release or rescan creates a new round, hence a new scan and (if eligible) a new rewritten copy; a producer never reuses another round's copy. A skipped rewrite (`no_urls`, `signed_smime`, `signed_pgp`, `encrypted`, `dkim_policy`, `too_large`, `structure_mismatch`, `parse_incomplete`, `disabled`, `direction`, `error`) means the original. The delivery-worker itself is unchanged: it reinjects whatever key the approval names (prefix-checked) and holds no token keys or URL tables. The planner counts `delivery_approvals_total{source, object="rewritten"|"original"}` (object from the key prefix). Implemented by `DevPassThroughApprover` (`apps/delivery-worker/src/approvals/dev-passthrough.approver.ts`, unit and integration tests); policy and quarantine release must reuse the same rule (and its tests) when they are built. Archiving and quarantine keep using the **original** EML.

## 19. Delivery status log (postfix-outbound → delivery-worker)

**IMPLEMENTED in step 2.3** ([design R9, R12](../plan/steps/05-routing.md), [line reference](../deployment/mail-routing.md#delivery-log-line-reference), [runbook](../runbooks/delivery-status-log.md)). The primary per-recipient status signal is Postfix's own delivery record, written by the `smtp` client after the remote server's reply to the final dot; postmaster DSN copies (§3) corroborate failures. Success DSNs are not used (they would go to the envelope sender).

| Aspect      | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| File        | `maillog_file = /var/log/postfix-outbound/maillog` (written only by `postlogd`, 0644), Compose bind mount `${POSTFIX_OUTBOUND_LOG_DIR}`, read-only in the delivery-worker; rotation by `postfix logrotate` (rename + gzip; the reader follows by device + inode and drains the old file first). Production: the same worker image as a sidecar on each postfix-outbound host (`DELIVERY_STATUS_LOG_ENABLED=true`, other roles off)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Lines       | Postfix 3.7 format `MMM DD HH:MM:SS out postfix/<service>[pid]: …` (no year: inferred from the UTC date). Binding `postfix/reinject-(inbound\|outbound)/smtpd: QID: client=…, orig_queue_id=dw1-<hex>` (only from the reinjection listeners' syslog names; a correlation only, never proof of queueing: a later session of a `pending`/`in_progress` row rebinds the token); `qmgr … (queue active)` → the reinjection `queued`, its recipients `queued` only when `nrcpt` covers the group (else each recipient by its own result line, matched by queue id or by address among the bound reinjection's recipients); `smtp … status=sent` with `dsn=2.x.x` → **`delivered`** (the only source of `delivered`); `status=deferred` → `deferred` (+ attempt when a remote attempt was made); `status=bounced` → `bounced`; `qmgr QID: from=<…>, status=expired, returned to sender` → `expired` for every non-final recipient; `bounce … sender non-delivery notification: DSNQID` → `dsn_queue_id`; `qmgr QID: removed` with a non-terminal delivery → `unknown`. Unbound queue ids are ignored |
| Cursor      | `stream_cursors` `postfix-log:<DELIVERY_STATUS_LOG_SOURCE_ID>` (file id, byte offset, last event time), committed **after** the batch's signals (at-least-once); one reader per source (`GET_LOCK('dw:postfix-log:<id>')`). While idle at the end of the file the last event time advances to `now − 30 s`. Unmatched lines of queue ids seen on a binding line are retried for `DELIVERY_STATUS_UNMATCHED_RETRY_SECONDS`; other queue ids are ignored after one lookup                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Recorder    | `recordDeliverySignal` (`@email-gateway/mail-routing`): resolve the delivery (id, queue id + address, or token + address), `FOR UPDATE`, insert the audit row `delivery_status_events` if `(delivery_id, signal_key)` is new (else `duplicate`: nothing else happens), decide with the lattice (`terminal` statuses never left except `unknown → terminal`; stale non-terminal signals and conflicting terminal signals kept with `applied = false`), then update `email_deliveries`, `delivery_attempts`, `email_recipients.status`, `email_events` (`delivery.<status>`), the outbox row `mail.delivery.status_changed.v1` and the message aggregate (`delivered`, `partially_delivered`, `failed`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Signal keys | `log\|{sourceId}\|{queueId}\|{address}\|{status}\|{logTimestamp}\|{dsn}`, `dsn\|…`, `reinject\|…`, `plan\|…`, `reconcile\|…`, `ratelimit\|…` (hex SHA-256): a re-read log line or a redelivered notice is a duplicate (`routing-signals.test.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Reconciler  | expired lease → `queued` (a recipient tied to the bound file), `pending` with the binding cleared (log passed the session), or row `failed` + recipients `unknown` (approval older than `DELIVERY_REINJECT_MAX_AGE_SECONDS`, never re-sent); recipients of a `queued` row never logged after `DELIVERY_RECONCILE_UNCONFIRMED_SECONDS` → re-planned (others confirmed) or `unknown` after the stale window; `queued`/`deferred` without a signal for `DELIVERY_STATUS_STALE_AFTER_SECONDS` → `unknown` (`status_signal_missing`); never a guessed outcome                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

## 20. MIME ingestion (email-worker)

**IMPLEMENTED in step 3.1** ([design](../plan/steps/06-mime-ingestion.md), [ADR-0022](adr/0022-mime-parsing-and-post-queue-ingestion.md)). Post-queue parsing of every stored message; the SMTP hot path (§3) is unchanged.

| Aspect       | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Input        | `mail.message.received.v1` (§6), consumer name **`email-worker.mime-parser`** (`processOnce` ledger key and cursor name), initial position `oldest`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Transport    | The shared outbox poller of `@email-gateway/common` (`EventFeed`) until step 3.2 WP4 replaces it with a consumer of the queue `email.received` (§4; hold retry, prefetch `MIME_CONCURRENCY`). Step 3.2 WP1 already dropped the `MIME_OUTBOX_*` keys from `.env.example` and Compose, so the poller runs on its built-in defaults until then. Cursor `outbox:email-worker.mime-parser` in `stream_cursors`; with `MIME_OUTBOX_SHARDS = n > 1` one cursor per shard, `outbox:<consumer>#<i>/<n>` (for example `outbox:email-worker.mime-parser#0/4`), and the lock `ew:outbox:<consumer>#<i>/<n>` |
| Stage record | **`email_message_parses`**, one row per message (`message_id, received_at` unique): `parsing` → `parsed` \| `parsed_with_defects` \| `parse_limit_exceeded` \| `parse_failed`, finalized once by a conditional update `WHERE status = 'parsing'`. `email_messages.status` is not changed (step 3.3 owns the lifecycle)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Output       | In one writer transaction: `email_headers`, `email_attachments`, `email_messages.subject` and `attachment_count`, one `email_events` row (`parsed` or `parse_failed`, codes and counts only) and one outbox event (`mail.message.parsed.v1` or `mail.message.parse_failed.v1`, §6). Bodies and attachments go to S3 under the part keys of §11                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Tenancy      | The envelope's `organizationId` must match the writer row of the message, and `eml_object_key` must start with `org/{organizationId}/`; otherwise nothing is parsed (`eml_key_mismatch` or no effect for a missing message)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Idempotency  | Ledger per event, the parse row per message, deterministic S3 keys and the unique keys of `email_headers` and `email_attachments`: a duplicate or concurrent event creates no second row, object or event                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

## 21. Message lifecycle (`@email-gateway/message-lifecycle`)

**DESIGNED in step 3.3** ([design](../plan/steps/08-message-lifecycle.md), [ADR-0025](adr/0025-message-lifecycle-state-machine.md)); the schema, the event contracts and topology v2 are IMPLEMENTED (WP1), the library and its producers are built by WP2–WP4. State diagram: [diagram 11](diagrams/11-message-state-machine.md); per-recipient delivery: [diagram 22](diagrams/22-delivery-lifecycle.md).

| Aspect      | Contract                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| States      | `email_messages.status` = the fifteen `MESSAGE_STATES` (`received`, `persisted`, `scanning`, `scanned`, `scan_failed`, `policy_evaluated`, `approved`, `quarantined`, `held_for_review`, `rejected`, `delivery_pending`, `delivery_retry`, `delivered`, `archived`, `expired`). Final: `delivered`, `rejected`, `archived`, `expired`; `archived` is reserved without a producer since step 8.1 (E25 retired, archive membership is a separate fact, §31). Partial and failed deliveries are reasons (`delivered/delivery_partial`, `rejected/delivery_failed`), not states |
| One door    | Every write of `status`, `status_changed_at`, `version`, `lifecycle_round`, `status_reason` and every `email_message_status_history` row goes through the library (a repository test enforces it, L5.9). Producers call it inside their own writer transaction                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| API         | `transitionMessage(tx, request)` (inside the caller's transaction, never retries) and `transitionMessageWithRetry(prisma, request, { timeoutMs })` (own transaction, retries deadlocks, lock waits and version conflicts); `initialLifecycle` + `writeInitialHistory` for the SMTP commit; `lockLifecycle`; `applyDerivedDeliveryState`; the pure `decideTransition` and `deriveDeliveryState`                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Request     | `{ message: { id, receivedAt, organizationId }, to, reason, actor: { role, service, type, id? }, transitionKey, causationEventId?, correlationId?, occurredAt?, expectedRound?, expectedVersion?, expectedFrom?, recordIgnored? }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Outcome     | `applied` (new version, round, history id, `stateEventId`) \| `duplicate` (`transition_key`, `same_state`) \| `stale` (`older_round`, `version_mismatch`, `overtaken`, `final_state`) \| `invalid` (`edge_not_allowed`, `actor_not_allowed`, `reason_not_allowed`, `unexpected_from`, `future_round`) \| `not_found` (no row, or another organization's message)                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Algorithm   | `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the message row; the transition key (SHA-256 hex) looked up in the history; the decision; `UPDATE … WHERE version = ?` (compare-and-set backstop); history row; `email_events` `lifecycle.<state>`; outbox `mail.message.state_changed.v1`. Stale and invalid outcomes of asynchronous producers write one `email_events` `lifecycle.ignored` row                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Ordering    | Rounds first (older round → stale, future round → invalid), then the expected version, then the edge table and the state rank (a target the message already passed → stale). Occurrence times are recorded (`occurred_at`), never compared.
